                                     Preface

TM 4-48.06, Airdrop of Supplies And Equipment, Rigging 5-Ton Truck, provides doctrinal guidance on the
approved procedures on how to prepare and rig a 5-ton cargo truck on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with
six G-11C cargo parachutes and other items of airdrop equipment to be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from
C-130 or C-17 aircraft.

The principal audience for TM 4-48.06 is all members of the profession of arms. Commanders and staffs of Army
headquarters serving as joint task force or multinational headquarters should also refer to applicable joint or
multinational doctrine concerning the range of military operations and joint or multinational forces. Trainers and
educators throughout the Army and the Air Force will also use this publication.

Commanders, staffs, and subordinates ensure that their decisions and actions comply with applicable United
States, international, and in some cases host-nation laws and regulations. Commanders at all levels ensure that
their Soldiers operate in accordance with the law of war and the rules of engagement. (See FM 27-10).

TM 4-48.06 uses joint terms where applicable. Selected joint and Army terms and definitions appear in both the
glossary and the text. For definitions shown in the text, the term is italicized and the number of the proponent
publication follows the definition. This publication is not the proponent for any Army terms.

TM 4-48.06 applies to Active Army, the Army National Guard/Army National Guard of the United States, United
States Army Reserve, United States Air Force, Air National Guard, and Air Force Reserve Command unless
otherwise noted.

The proponent of TM 4-48.06 is the United States Army Combined Arms Support Command. The preparing
agency is the Aerial Delivery and Field Services Department, United States Army Quartermaster School. Send
comments and recommendations on a DA Form 2028 (Recommended Changes to Publications and Blank Forms).

Army personnel, send your comments on DA Form 2028 to:
          Director
          Aerial Delivery and Field Services Department
          U.S. Army Quartermaster School
          710 Adams Avenue
          Fort Lee, Virginia 23801-1502

Or submit an electronic DA Form 2028 by e-mail to: usarmy.lee.tradoc.mbx.leee-cascom-doctrine@mail.mil.

Air Force personnel, send your comments on AF Form 847 (Recommendation for Change of Publication) through
your respective command weapons and tactics to:

          Headquarters
          Air Mobility Command (AMC/A3DT)
          402 Scott Drive, Unit 3AI
          Scott Air Force Base, Illinois 62225-5302

Air Force personnel in Special Operations Command, send your reports on AF Form 847 to Headquarters Air
Mobility Command (AMC/A3DT). Air Mobility Command will consolidate and forward changes to:

          Director
          Aerial Delivery and Field Services Department
          U.S. Army Quartermaster School
          710 Adams Avenue
          Fort Lee, Virginia 23801-1502

Also send an information copy of AF Form 847 to:
          406 SCMS/GULAC
          460 Richard Ray Blvd
          STE 200
          Robins Air Force Base, Georgia 31098-1813

     Publication of TM 4-48.06/TO 13C7-2-481 (FM 10-526/TO 13C7-2-481) Airdrop of Supplies and
     Equipment: Rigging 5-Ton Trucks supersedes FM 10-526, Airdrop of Supplies and Equipment: Rigging 5-
     Ton Trucks, 2 May 1985.

     This special revision does not integrate any changes in Army doctrine since 2 May 1985 and does alter the
     publication’s original references. For the status of official Department of the Army (DA) publications, consult
     DA Pam 25-30, Consolidated Index of Army Publications and Blank Forms, at

                                                 Chapter 1

                      Description of Rigged Items

         The description and unrigged data for the items covered in this manual are described
         in this chapter.

THE M813 OR M54, 5-TON CARGO TRUCK

     1-1. The M813 is rigged on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo parachutes and other
     items of airdrop equipment. The weight and dimensions of the truck are given in Chapter 2, Table 2-1 on
     page 2-1. This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17 aircraft. The M813 truck
     is shown throughout chapter 3. Figure 3-1 on page 3-1 shows the unrigged M813 truck. The truck you are
     rigging may vary slightly from the one shown, depending on the make and model. Adapt these procedures as
     necessary to rig your truck.

THE M817 OR M51, 5-TON DUMP TRUCK

     1-2. The M817 is rigged on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo parachutes and other
     items of airdrop equipment. This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17 aircraft.
     The M817 truck is shown throughout chapter 4. Figure 4-1 on page 4-1 shows the unrigged M817 truck. The
     truck you are rigging may vary slightly from the one shown, depending on the make and model. Adapt these
     procedures as necessary to rig your truck.

THE M925, 5-TON CARGO TRUCK

     1-3. The M925 is rigged on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo parachutes and other
     items of airdrop equipment. The M925 truck with winch weighs 22,360 pounds. Its height is 116 inches,
     reducible to 91 inches. The width of the truck is 98 inches. The length of the truck with winch is 329 inches.
     This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17 aircraft. Figures 5-1 and 5-2 on
     pages 5-1 and 5-2 show the unrigged M925 truck equipped with a winch and a hardtop cab cover. The truck
     you are rigging may vary slightly from the one shown, depending on the make and model. Adapt these
     procedures as necessary to rig your truck.

THE M925A1, 5-TON CARGO TRUCK

     1-4. The M925A1 is rigged on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo parachutes and
     other items of airdrop equipment. The M925A1 truck with winch weighs 22,360 pounds. Its height is 116
     inches, reducible to 91 inches. The width of the truck is 98 inches. The length of the truck with winch is 320
     inches. This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17 aircraft. The truck you are
     rigging may vary slightly from the one shown, depending on the make and model. Adapt these procedures as
     necessary to rig your truck.

THE M929, 5-TON DUMP TRUCK

     1-5. The M929 is rigged on a 28-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo parachutes and other
     items of airdrop equipment. The M929 truck weights 24,250 pounds. Its height is 120 ½ inches, reducible to
     92 ½ inches. The width of the truck is 95 inches. The length of the truck is 273 inches. This truck may be
     delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17 aircraft. Figure 7-1 on page 7-1 shows the unrigged
     M929 truck equipped with a bumper extension and a cab cover. The truck you rigging may vary slightly from
     the one shown, depending on the make and model. Adapt these procedures as necessary to rig your truck.

15 March 2016  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481  1-1

Chapter 1

SPECIAL CONSIDERATIONS FOR THIS MANUAL ARE GIVEN BELOW.

     1-6. The loads covered in this manual may include hazardous materials as defined in AFMAN 24-204/TM
     38-250/NAVSUP PUB 505/MCO P4030.19J/DLAI 4145.3. If included, the hazardous materials must be
     packaged, marked, and labeled as required by AFMAN 24-204/TM 38-250/NAVSUP PUB 505/MCO
     P4030.19J/DLAI 4145.3. A copy of this manual must be available to the joint airdrop inspectors during the
     before- and after-loading inspection.

                                                                                  Rigging Information for Type V Airdrop Platform

SPECIAL CONSIDERATIONS

     2-2. Special considerations for the type V platform are given below.
              These loads may include hazardous material as defined in AFMAN 24-204/TM 38-250/NAVSUP
                  PUB 505/MCO P4030.19J/DLAI 4145.3. If hazardous material is included, it must be packaged,
                  marked, and labeled as required by AFMAN 24-204/TM 38-250/NAVSUP PUB 505/MCO
                  P4030.19J/DLAI 4145.3.
              When rigging the truck for low-velocity airdrop, use only type XXVI nylon webbing suspension
                  slings, the M-2 cargo parachute release assembly, and the extraction force transfer coupler.
              A copy of this manual must be available to the joint airdrop inspectors during the before and after-
                  loading inspections.

MODIFYING TRUCK

     2-3. The truck must be modified by qualified maintenance personnel before it is delivered to the rigging
     site. The following modifications must be made to all trucks to be rigged for low-velocity airdrop.

              If the truck is NOT equipped with a winch, mainframe extension assemblies MUST be installed.
                  Figure 2-1 shows the mainframe extension assemblies installed.

              The standard front lifting shackle brackets MUST be replaced with heavy-duty, 1 1/8-inch-thick,
                  front lifting shackle brackets. Figure 2-2 on page 2-4 shows the 1 1/8-inch thick brackets installed.

              Access holes must be made in the body of the trucks. Figure 2-3 on page 2-5 shows the location
                  of the access holes in the body of the cargo truck. Figure 2-4 on page 2-6 shows the location of
                  the access holes in the body of the dump truck.

Figure 2-1. Mainframe extension assemblies installed on the front of the truck

15 March 2016  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481                                        2-3

                                                 CAUTION

                                                                                  Rigging Information for Type V Airdrop Platform

ACCOMPANYING LOAD

     2-4. An accompanying load may be rigged as art of any load listed in this manual.
              The accompanying load MUST-
                   Be stowed in the body of the truck.
                   Meet the requirements and the restrictions given in TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA
                  SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.
              The accompanying load MUST NOT-
                   Exceed the height of the cab when the cargo truck is rigged.
                   Exceed the height of the spare tire when the dump truck is rigged
                   Increase the height of the completely rigged load.
                   Interfere with or restrict the suspension slings.
                   Cause the total suspended weight to exceed 25,270 pounds.

15 March 2016  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481  2-7

                                                 Chapter 3

 Rigging the M813 or M54, 5-Ton Cargo Truck On A Type V
                                    Platform

DESCRIPTION OF LOAD

     3-1. The M813 of M54, 5-ton cargo truck is rigged on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C
     cargo parachutes and other items of airdrop equipment. The weight and dimensions of the truck are given in
     Chapter 2, Table 2-1 on pages 2-1 and 2-2. This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130
     or C-17 aircraft. The M813 truck is shown throughout this chapter. Figure 3-1 shows the unrigged M813
     truck. The truck you are rigging may vary slightly from the one shown, depending on the make and model.
     Adapt these procedures as necessary to rig your truck.

PREPARING PLATFORM

     3-2. Prepare a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform as described below.
              Inspecting the platform. Inspect, or assemble and inspect, the platform according to TM 10-1670-
                  268-20 & P/TO 13C7-52-22.

             Note: If the platform must be assembled, install the suspension links when assembling the
             platform. See Figure 3-2 on page 3-3 for the location of the suspension links.

              Installing suspension links. Install the suspension links as described in Figure 3-2.
              Installing tandem links. Install a tandem link on the front of each rail as shown in Figure 3-3 on

                  page 3-4.
              Attaching and numbering clevises. Attach and number 36 clevises as shown in Figure 3-3.
              Labeling and numbering tiedown. Label and number the tiedown rings as shown in Figure 3-3.

             Notes 1. The nose bumper may or may not be installed.
                    2. Measurements given in this section are from the front edge of the platform, NOT from
                        the front edge of the nose bumper.

3-2            TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481       15 March 2016

Chapter 3

REMOVING TRUCK COMPONENTS

     3-4. Remove the cab top cover, cab top frame, cargo body cover, mirror assemblies, exhaust stack, spare
     wheel assembly, side rack troop seats, body side racks, and bow and stack assemblies according to TM 9-
     2320-260-10 and TM 9-2320-260-20.

PREPARING TRUCK

3-5. Prepare the truck as shown in Figure 3-15 on the following pages and as described below.
        Make sure the fuel tank is not more than ½ full.
        Make sure the fire extinguisher is charged and the safety pin is secure. Pad the fire extinguisher,
             and secure it to the vehicle.
        Fill the toolbox and the tool stowage box with scrap honeycomb or cellulose wadding.
        Place the pioneer tools in their links, and fasten the retaining straps. Tie the tools in place with
             type III nylon cord.

                 3                          2

                    4 1

① Wrap a ¾-by18- by 60-inch piece of plywood in cellulose wadding and tape the wadding in place.

     Place the plywood on the hood of the truck.

② Fold the windshield down on the plywood.

③ Place a 24-by 60-inch piece of honeycomb on top of the windshield. Tape the edges of

     honeycomb with tape.

④ Pass the end of a 15-foot tiedown strap around the left front fender brace and through its own

     D-ring. Pull the free end tight, and lay the strap across the honeycomb.

                                              Figure 3-15. Truck prepared

11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

① Place a spacer from a four-point link assembly in one end loop of a 16-foot (4-loop), type XXVI

     nylon webbing sling. Fit a large suspension clevis on a front lifting shackle, and bolt the end
     loop with the spacer to the clevis.

② Install another 16-foot (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing sling on the other front lifting shackle

     as described in step 1 above.

③ Fit a large suspension clevis on each year suspension bracket. Place a spacer from a four-

     point link assembly in one end loop of two 12-foot (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing slings.
     Bolt the end loop of one sling with the spacer to each large suspension clevis on the rear
     suspension brackets.

     and a load binder to secure each end of the strap to the inverted clevises according to TM 4-
     48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

     suspension slings and the cargo parachutes according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA
     SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5. Center the release assembly on the top of the load.

Chapter 3

INSTALLING EXTRACTION SYSTEM

     3-18. Install the extraction force transfer coupler extraction system as shown in Figure 3-41 on the following
     pages.

      1  2

① Attach the type V Extraction Force Transfer Actuator (EFTA) mounting brackets to the rear

     mounting holes in the left platform rail.

② Install the actuator with a 24-foot cable to the EFTA mounting brackets according to TM 4-

     48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

                                      Figure 3-41. Extraction system installed

3-56  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481  15 March 2016


---

               Rigging the M813 or M54, 5-Ton Cargo Truck On A Type V Platform

               5

               4
                                           3

③ Use a 5-inch latch assembly adapter, and attach the latch assembly to the extraction bracket

     according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.
     Make sure the locking nut hole faces toward the left side of the platform.

④ Connect one end of a 9-foot (2-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing sling (deployment line) to the

     top spacer of the link assembly. Connect the free end to the center large suspension clevis on
     the 3-foot clustering slings.

⑤ Fold the excess deployment line, and secure the folds in place with tape to type I, ¼-inch

     cotton webbing.

Chapter 3

PLACING EXTRACTION PARACHUTE

     3-19. Select the extraction parachute and extraction line needed using the extraction line requirements table
     in TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5. Place the extraction
     parachute and line on the load for installation in the aircraft.

INSTALLING PROVISIONS FOR EMERGENCY RESTRAINTS

     3-20. Select and install the provisions for the emergency aft restraints according to the emergency aft
     restraint requirements table in TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO
     13C7-1-5.

MARKING RIGGED LOAD

     3-21. Mark the rigged load according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV
     1/TO 13C7-1-5, and as shown in Figure 3-42. Complete Shipper's Declaration for Dangerous Goods If the
     load varies from the one shown, the weight, height, center of balance, and parachute requirements must be
     recomputed.

EQUIPMENT REQUIRED

    3-22.Use the equipment listed in Table 3-1 to rig this load.

                                                 Chapter 4

    Rigging M817 or M51 5-Ton Dump Truck on a Type V

DESCRIPTION OF LOAD

     4-1. The M817 or M51, 5-ton dump truck is rigged on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C
     cargo parachutes and other items of airdrop equipment. This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop
     from C-130 or C-17 aircraftThe M817 truck is shown throughout this chapter. Figure 4-1 shows the unrigged
     M817 truck. The truck you are rigging may vary slightly from the one shown, depending on the make and
     model. Adapt these procedures as necessary to rig your truck

Chapter 4

PREPARING PLATFORM

     4-2. Prepare a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform as described below.
              Inspecting the platform. Inspect, or assemble and inspect, the platform according to TM 10-1670-
                  268-20 & P/TO 13C7-52-22.

             Note. If the platform must be assembled, install the suspension links when assembling the
             platform. See Figure 4-2 for the location of the suspension links.

            Installing suspension links. Install the suspension links as described in Figure 4-2.
            Installing tandem links. Install a tandem link on the front of each rail as shown in Figure 4-3.
            Attaching and numbering clevises. Attach and number 46 clevises as shown in Figure 4-3.
            Labeling and numbering tiedowns. Label and number the tiedown rings as shown in Figure 4-3.

           Notes 1. The nose bumper may or may not be installed.
                   2. Measurements given in this section are from the front edge of the platform, NOT from
                      the front edge of the nose bumper.

Note. This drawing is not drawn to scale.

           REAR                                            5

                                                                      LEFT
                                4

     3

              RIGHT
                             2

                                                                            FRONT

                                                               1

① Remove any bushings, bolts, and tandem links that may have been installed in holes 1 through 11 on the

      right rail.

② Place a suspension link in the front of the right rail with the flat portion to the front of the rail. Slide the link

      along the rail until the hole in the link align with rail holes 9, 10, and 11. Bolt the link in place with the
      bushing bolts.

③ Remove any bushings, bolts, and tandem links that may have been installed in holes 38 through 48 on

      the right rail.

④ Place a suspension link on the rear of the right rail with the flat portion to rear of the rail. Slide the link

      along the rail until the holes in the link align with rail holes 38, 39, and 40. Bolt the link in place with the
     bushing bolts.

⑤ Install two suspension links on the left rail, adapting the procedures in steps 1 through 4 above.

Steps:

     1 Install a tandem link on the front of each platform side rail using holes 1, 2, and 3.
     2 Install a clevis on bushings 1 and 4 of each front tandem link.
     3 Install a clevis on bushings 1 on each front suspension link.
     4 Install a clevis on bushings 2 and 4 on each rear suspension link.
     5 Starting at the front of each platform side rail, install clevises on each platform side rail

          using the bushings bolted on holes 4, 18, 19, 20, 22, 24, 26, 27, 33, 34, 36, 42, 43, 44, 45,
          46, 47, and 48.
     6 Start at the front of the platform, number the clevises bolted to the right side from 1 through
          23 and those bolted to the left side from 1A through 23A.
     7 Label the two rows of tiedown rings in the first 11 panels A and B from right to left. Label
          the two rows of tiedown rings in the last panel A, B, C, and D from right to left. Starting at
          the front of the platform, number the rows of tiedown rings 1 through 12.

PREPARING TRUCK

     4-7. Prepare the truck as shown in Figure 4-19 on the following pages and as described below.
           Make sure the fuel tank is not more than ½ full.
           Make sure the fire extinguisher is charged and the safety pin is secured. Pad the fire extinguisher, and
                secure it to the vehicle.
           Fill the toolbox and the tool stowage box with scrap honeycomb or cellulose wadding.

                        3

               1

                     2                       6

                  4                          5

① Cover the exhaust pipe opening with cellulose wadding. Tape the wadding in place.

     place. Place the plywood on the hood of the truck.

③ Fold the windshield down on the plywood, and place a 24- by 60-inch piece of honeycomb on

     top of the windshield.

④ Pass the free end of a 15-foot tiedown strap around the right front fender brace and through its

     own D-ring. Pull the free end tight, and lay the strap across the honeycomb.

     own D-ring. Pull the free end of the strap tight.

                                                 Chapter 5

   Rigging M925 5-Ton Cargo Truck on a Type V Platform

DESCRIPTION OF LOAD

     5-1. The M925, 5-ton cargo truck is rigged on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo
     parachutes and other items of airdrop equipment. The M925 truck with winch weighs 22,360 pounds. Its
     height is 116 inches, reducible to 91 inches. The width of the truck is 98 inches. The length of the truck with
     winch is 329 inches. This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17 aircraft. Figures
     5-1 and 5-2 show the unrigged M925 truck equipped with a winch and a hardtop cab cover. The truck you
     are rigging may vary slightly from the one shown, depending on the make and model. Adapt these procedures
     as necessary to rig your truck.

               Figure 5-1. Left side of unrigged M925, 5-ton cargo truck

PREPARING PLATFORM

     5-2. Prepare a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform as described below.
              Inspecting Platform. Inspect, or assemble and inspect, the platform according to TM 10-1670-
                  268-20&P/TO 13C7-52-22.

          Note. If the platform must be assembled, install the suspension links when assembling the platform.

              Installing Suspension Links. Install the suspension links as described in Figure 5-3 on page 5-4.
              Installing Tandem Links. Install a tandem link on the front of each rail as shown in Figure 5-4

                  on page 5-5.
              Attaching and Numbering Clevises. Attach and number 38 clevises as shown in Figure 5-4.
              Labeling and Numbering Tiedown Rings. Label and number the tiedown rings as shown in

                  Figure 5-4.

          Notes. 1. The nose bumper may or may not be installed.
                    2. Measurements given in this section are from the front edge of the platform, NOT from
                       the front edge of the nose bumper.

     the link along the rail until the holes in the link align with rails holes 9, 10, and 11. Bolt the link in
     place with bushing bolts.

③ Remove bushings, bolts, and any tandem links that may have been installed in holes 38 through

     48 on the right rail.

④ Place a suspension link in the rear of the right rail with the flat portion to the rear of the rail. Slide

     the link along the rail until the holes in the link align with rail holes 38, 39, and 40. Bolt the link in
     place with the bushing bolts.

Steps:

          the bushings bolted on holes 5, 17, 18, 21, 22, , 26, 30, 32, 42, 43, 44, 45, 46, 47, and 48.

          and those bolted to the left side from 1A through 19A.
     7. Label the two rows of tiedown rings in the first 11 panels A and B from right to left. Label the
          two rows of tiedown rings in the last panel A, B, C, and D from right to left. Starting at the front
          of the platform, number the rows of tiedown rings 1 through 11.

REMOVING TRUCK COMPONENTS

     5-4. Remove the hardtop cab cover, cargo body cover, mirror assemblies, exhaust stack, air cleaner stack,
     side rack troop seats, body side racks, and bow and stack assemblies according to TM 9-2320-272-10.

PREPARING TRUCK

     5-5. Prepare the truck as shown in Figure 5-22 and as described below.
              Reduce the tire pressure in all tires to 28 pounds per square inch.
              Make sure the fuel tank is not more than ½ full.
                                                                                    REVERSED LIFTING SHACKLE

     ② Tie the winch chain hook to the rear bar of the winch with ½-inch tubular nylon webbing.
     ③ Secure the grill retainer bar in the retainer bag link of tie the grill retainer bar to the front of the

            truck with ½-inch tubular nylon webbing.

     ④ Remove the front lifting shackles from their links. Reverse the shackles, and fasten them to

            the links with their pins. Place the safety pins in the holes in the ends of the retaining pins.

INSTALLING SUSPENSION SLINGS AND ANTITUMBLE SLINGS

     5-12. Install the suspension slings and antitumble slings as shown in Figure 5-42 on the following pages.

                    3   4                         5
               7            2                             1

                                       6
                       8

① Place the bell portion of a large clevis through the loop of a 20-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon

     webbing sling. Bolt the clevis to the right front suspension link.

② Place the bell portion of a large clevis through the loop of a 20-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon

     webbing sling. Bolt the clevis to the left front suspension link.

③ Using two 16-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing slings, adapt the procedures in steps 1 and

     2 above and install the rear suspension slings to the rear suspension links.

④ Pull the suspension slings tight above the load.

⑤ Wrap a 15- by 36-inch piece of felt around each front suspension sling 30 inches from the large

     clevis. Tape the felt in place.

⑥ Tie each front suspension sling to the front suspension sling spreader with a turn of ½-inch

     tubular nylon webbing.

⑦ Wrap a 15- by 36-inch piece of felt around each rear suspension sling 30 inches from the large

     clevis. Tape the felt in place.

⑧ Tie each rear suspension sling to the side board ring tiedown with a single turn of ½-inch tubular

     nylon webbing.

STOWING CARGO PARACHUTES

                                                  NOTICE OF EXCEPTION
                      The parachute requirements and restraint straps in this paragraph are not in
                      accordance with TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010
                      REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5. Six G-11C cargo parachutes and type X nylon webbing
                      used as restraint straps are authorized to be used with this load.
     5-14. Stow six G-11C cargo parachutes on the truck as shown in Figure 5-44.

                  1

               5                          2
                               3              4

① Prepare and position six G-11C cargo parachutes in the rear of the truck as shown. Each

     parachuted requires a 120-foot riser extension. Make sure the riser extensions meet the
     requirements and restrictions in TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV
     1/TO 13C7-1-5.

② Bolt a load tiedown clevis to clevises 14 and 14A in an inverted manner. Install a 10-yard, type X

     nylon webbing parachutes between the side body and the truck bed. Use a D-ring and a load
     binder to secure each end of the strap to the inverted clevises according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-
     11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

③ Install a 10-yard, type X nylon webbing parachute restraint strap over the bottom of the

     parachutes between the side body and the truck bed. Use a D-ring and a load binder to secure
     each end of the strap to clevises 19 and 19A according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA
     SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

④ Bolt a load tiedown clevis to clevises 18 and 18A in an inverted manner. Install a 10-yard, type X

     nylon webbing parachute restraint strap over the center of the cargo parachutes between the side
     body and the truck bed. Use a D-ring and a load binder to secure each end of the strap to the
     inverted clevises according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV
     1/TO 13C7-1-5.

⑤ Install two multicut parachute release straps according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA

     SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

     positioned in step 1 above.

     webbing.

                                                 Chapter 6

 Rigging M925A1 5-Ton Cargo Truck on a Type V Platform

DESCRIPTION OF LOAD

     6-1. The M925A1, 5-ton cargo truck is rigged on a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo
     parachutes and other items of airdrop equipment. The M925A1 truck with winch weighs 22,360 pounds. Its
     height is 116 inches, reducible to 91 inches. The width of the truck is 98 inches. The length of the truck with
     winch is 320 inches. This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17 aircraft. The
     truck you are rigging may vary slightly from the one shown in figure 6-1 and 6-2, depending on the make
     and model. Adapt these procedures as necessary to rig your truck.

               Figure 6-1. Right Side of Unrigged M925A1, 5-Ton Cargo Truck

15 March 2016  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481                                     6-1


---

Chapter 6

           Figure 6-2. Front and Rear Views of Unrigged M925A1, 5-Ton Cargo Truck

6-2        TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481  15 March 2016

                                                                     Rigging M925A1 5-Ton Cargo Truck on a Type V Platform

PREPARING PLATFORM

     6-2. Prepare a 24-foot, type V airdrop platform as described below and depicted in figures 6-3 and 6-4 on
     the following pages.

              Inspecting Platform. Inspect, or assemble and inspect, the platform according to TM 10-1670-
                  268-20&P/TO 13C7-52-22.

              Installing Suspension Links. Install the suspension links as described in Figure 6-3.
              Installing Tandem Links. Install a tandem link on the front of each rail as shown in Figure 6-4.
              Attaching and Numbering Clevises. Attach and number 38 clevises as shown in Figure 6-4.
              Labeling and Numbering Tiedown Rings. Label and number the tiedown rings as shown in

                  Figure 6-4.

          Notes. 1. The nose bumper may or may not be installed.
                    2. Measurements given in this section are from the front edge of the platform, NOT from
                       the front edge of the nose bumper.

15 March 2016  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481  6-3


---

Chapter 6
 Note. This is drawing is not drawn to scale.

① Remove bushings, bolts, and any tandem links that may have been installed in holes 1 through

     11 on the right rail.

② Place a suspension link in the front of the right rail with the flat portion to the front of the rail. Slide

     the link along the rail until the holes in the link align with rails holes 9, 10, and 11. Bolt the link in
     place with bushing bolts.

③ Remove bushings, bolts, and any tandem links that may have been installed in holes 38 through

     48 on the right rail.

④ Place a suspension link in the rear of the right rail with the flat portion to the rear of the rail. Slide

     the link along the rail until the holes in the link align with rail holes 38, 39, and 40. Bolt the link in
     place with the bushing bolts.

⑤ Install two suspension links on the left rail, adapting the procedures in steps 1 through 4 above.

                                       Figure 6-3. Suspension Links Installed

Steps:
     1 Install a tandem link on the front of each platform side rail using holes 1, 2, and 3.
     2 Install a clevis on bushings 1 and 3 of each front tandem link.
     3 Install a clevis on bushings 1 on each front suspension link.
     4 Install a clevis on bushings 2 and 4 on each rear suspension link.
     5 Starting at the front of each platform side rail, install clevises on each platform side rail
          using the bushings bolted on holes 5, 17, 18, 21, 22, , 26, 30, 32, 42, 43, 44, 45, 46, 47,
          and 48.
     6 Start at the front of the platform, number the clevises bolted to the right side from 1 through
          20 and those bolted to the left side from 1A through 20A.
     7 Label the two rows of tiedown rings in the first 11 panels A and B from right to left. Label
          the two rows of tiedown rings in the last panel A, B, C, and D from right to left. Starting at
          the front of the platform, number the rows of tiedown rings 1 through 11.

REMOVING TRUCK COMPONENTS

     6-4. Remove the hardtop cab cover, cargo body cover, hood handle, mirror assemblies, exhaust stack, air
     cleaner stack, side rack troop seats, body side racks, and bow and stack assemblies according to TM 9-2320-
     272-10.

PREPARING TRUCK

     6-5. Prepare the truck as shown in Figure 6-24 on pages 6-25 through 6-39 and as described below.
              Reduce the tire pressure in all tires to 28 pound per square inch.
              Make sure the fuel tank is not more than ½ full.

     truck with ½-inch tubular nylon webbing.

     through the mainframe. Place the front special tiedown provision mounting block on the bolts

POSITIONING TRUCK

     6-8. Position the truck as described below.
              Install four 16-foot (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing slings as shown in Figure 6-29.
              Position the truck on the honeycomb stacks as shown in Figure 6-30.
             Note. Other slings of equal or greater strength may be used to lift the truck.

 Note. Pad or tape the area where the slings touch the truck to protect the slings.

      2                                                                                                4
                   1                                                                                        3

① Pass the end of a 16-foot (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing sling between the side body and the

     body floor of the truck. Attach the end of the sling to the spring saddle with a screw-pin clevis.

② Install another sling on the other side of the truck as described in step 1 above.
③ Attach the end of a 16-foot (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing sling to a front lifting shackle with a

     large clevis.

④ Install another sling on the other side of the truck as described in step 3 above.

Note. After slings are attached to the truck and the lifting device, place the transmission lever in
neutral and release the brakes.

INSTALLING SUSPENSION SLINGS AND ANTITUMBLE SLINGS

     6-14. Install the suspension slings and antitumble slings as shown in Figure 6-49.

                  4

               3     2

                                            8
                                                                                                  6

                                                7
                                                                                                 5

                                                                                               1

① Place the bell portion of a large clevis through the loop of a 20-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon

     webbing sling. Bolt the clevis to the right front suspension link.

② Place the bell portion of a large clevis through the loop of a 20-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon

     webbing sling. Bolt the clevis to the left front suspension link.

③ Using two 16-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing slings, adapt the procedures in steps 1 and

     2 above and install the rear suspension slings to the rear suspension links.

④ Pull the suspension slings tight above the load.
⑤ Wrap a 15- by 36-inch piece of felt around each front suspension sling 30 inches from the large

     clevis. Tape the felt in place.

⑥ Tie each front suspension sling to the front suspension sling spreader with a single turn of ½-inch

     tubular nylon webbing.

⑦ Wrap a 15- by 36-inch piece of felt around each rear suspension sling 30 inches from the large

     clevis. Tape the felt in place.

⑧ Tie each rear suspension sling to the side board ring tiedown with a single turn of ½-inch tubular

     nylon webbing.

STOWING CARGO PARACHUTES

     6-16. Stow six G-11C cargo parachutes on the truck as shown in Figure 6-53.

                                                   CAUTION

                      These parachute restraint straps must be tight. Recheck each strap
                      after transportation and before loading the aircraft.

               1  2  3

                                                2
                                                            4

① Prepare and position six G-11C cargo parachutes in the rear of the truck as shown. Each

     parachute requires a 120-foot riser extension. Make sure the riser extensions meet the
     requirements and restrictions in TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV
     1/TO 13C7-1-5.

② Install a type X nylon webbing parachute restraint strap over the center of the parachutes

     between the side body and the truck bed. Use a D-ring and a load binder to secure each end of
     the strap to the last tiedown provision on the frame of the truck. Safety the load binders to the
     load using type III nylon cord.

③ Install a type X nylon webbing parachute restraint strap through the top row of parachutes,

     between the side body and the truck bed behind the second to the last hinge. Use a D-ring and a
     load binder to secure each end of the strap to the second from the last tiedown provision on the
     frame of the truck. Safety the load binders to the load using type III nylon cord.

④ Attach a type V platform clevis to the tiedown clevis under each bumperette. Then install a type

     X nylon webbing parachute restraint strap through the top row of parachutes, between the side
     body and the truck bed behind the last hinge. Use a D-ring and a load binder to secure each end
     of the strap to the type V clevis installed above. Safety the load binders to the load using type III
     nylon cord.

                                      Figure 6-53. Cargo Parachutes Stowed

                                                                        1

                                                                    3

 ① Attach the type V Extraction Force Transfer Actuator (EFTA) mounting brackets to the rear

       48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

       the locking nut hole facing toward the left side of the platform.

       spacer of the link assembly. Connect the free end to the center large suspension clevis on the 3-
       foot clustering slings.

       webbing.

                                                 Chapter 7

   Rigging M929, 5-Ton Dump Truck on a Type V Platform

DESCRIPTION OF LOAD

     7-1. The M929, 5-ton dump truck is rigged on a 28-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo
     parachutes and other items of airdrop equipment. The M929 truck weights 24,250 pounds. Its height is 120
     ½ inches, reducible to 92 ½ inches. The width of the truck is 95 inches. The length of the truck is 273 inches.
     This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17 aircraft. Figure 7-1 shows the
     unrigged M929 truck equipped with a bumper extension and a cab cover. The truck you rigging may vary
     slightly from the one shown, depending on the make and model. Adapt these procedures as necessary to rig
     your truck.

               Figure 7-1. Right Side of Unrigged M929, 5-Ton Dump Truck

15 March 2016  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481                                  7-1

Chapter 7

PREPARING PLATFORM

     7-2. Prepare a 28-foot, type V airdrop platform as described below.
              Inspecting PlatformInspect, or assemble and inspect, the platform according to TM 10-1670-268-
                  20 & P/TO 13C7-52-22.

             Note. If the platform must be assembled, install the suspension links when assembling the
             platformSee Figure 7-2 for the location of the suspension links.

            Installing suspension linksInstall the suspension links as described in Figure 7-2.
            Installing tandem linksInstall a tandem link on the front of each rail as shown in Figure 7-3.
            Attaching and numbering clevisesAttach and number 46 clevises as shown in Figure 7-3.
            Labeling and Numbering tiedownLabel and number the tiedown rings as shown in Figure 7-3.

           Note. 1.The nose bumper may or may not be installed.
                  2. Measurements given in this section are from the front edge of the platform, NOT from

           the front edge of the nose bumper.

① Remove any bushings, bolts, and tandem links that may have been installed in holes 1 through

     11 on the right rail.

② Place a suspension link in the front of the right rail with the flat portion to the front of the rail.

     Slide the link along the rail until the hole in the link align with rail holes 9, 10, and 11. Bolt the
     link in place with the bushing bolts.

③ Remove any bushings, bolts, and tandem links that may have been installed in holes 38

     through 56 on the right rail.

④ Place a suspension link on the rear of the right rail with the flat portion to rear of the rail. Slide

     the link along the rail until the holes in the link align with rail holes 38, 39, and 40. Bolt the link
     in place with the bushing bolts.

⑤ Install two suspension links on the left rail, adapting the procedures in steps 1 through 4

     above.

5. Install a tandem link on the front of each platform side rail using holes 1, 2, and 3.

     45, 47, 48, and 56.

11. Label the two rows of tiedown rings in the first 11 panels A and B from right to left. Label
     the two rows of tiedown rings in the last panel A, B, C, and D from right to left. Starting at
     the front of the platform, number the rows of tiedown rings 1 through 14.

       tubular nylon webbing.

       clevis. Tape the felt in place.

       010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5. Pass the ends of the strap through bushings 55 and 55A and secure

                                                 Chapter 8

 Rigging M929A2, 5-Ton Dump Truck on a 32-Foot Type V
                  Platform for Low-Velocity Airdrop

DESCRIPTION OF LOAD

     8-1. The M929A2, 5-ton dump truck is rigged on a 32-foot, type V airdrop platform with six G-11C cargo
     parachutes and other items of airdrop equipment. The M929 truck weights 23,600 pounds reducible to 22,
     640 pounds. Its height is 124 ½ inches, reducible to 93 ½ inches. The width of the truck is 95 inches. The
     length of the truck is 273 inches. This truck may be delivered by low-velocity airdrop from C-130 or C-17
     aircraft.

PREPARING PLATFORM

     8-2. Prepare a 32-foot, type V airdrop platform as shown in Figure 8-1.

15 March 2016  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481  8-1


---

Chapter 8

           CLEVISES 25A THROUGH 1A    FRONT
                          LEFT

REAR

                                                             RIGHT

                                              CLEVISES 27 THROUGH 1

Steps:
     30. Inspect or assemble and inspect a 32-foot, type V airdrop platform as outlined in TM 4-
          48.02/MCRP 4-11J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.
     31. Staring at the front of the platform. Attach tandem links to bushing holes 1, 2, and 3.
     32. Attach the first suspension link to bushing holes 10, 11, and 12 on each side of the platform.
          Attach the second suspension link to holes 26, 27, and 28 on each side of the platform.
          Attach the third suspension link to holes 37, 38, and 39 on each side of the platform. Attach
          the fourth suspension link to holes 53, 54, and 55 on each side of the platform.
     33. Install a clevis on bushings 1and 2 of each front tandem link.
     34. Install a clevis on bushings 2, 3, and 4 on the second suspension links.
     35. Install a clevis on bushings 1, 2, 3, and 4 on the fourth suspension links.
     36. Starting at the front of each platform side rail, install clevises on each platform side rail using
          the bushings bolted on holes 7, 8, 13, 14, 15, 17, 22, 30, 41, 42, 43, 45, 48, 57, 60, 61, 62.
          and 64.
     37. Start at the front of the platform, number the clevises bolted to the right side from 1 through
          25 and those bolted to the left side from 1A through 25A.
     38. . Starting at the front of the platform side rails, number the clevises attached to the right side
          rail 1 through 27 and 1A through 27A to the left rail.

REMOVING TRUCK COMPONENTS

     8-4. Remove the hardtop cab cover and canvas top, cargo body cover, mirror assemblies, air intake cap
     windshield, spare tire and tray holder, and exhaust stack assemblies according to TM 9-2320-272-10.

PREPARING TRUCK

     8-5. Prepare the truck as shown in Figure 8-16 and as described below.
              Reduce the tire pressure in all tires to 12 pounds per square inch.
              Make sure the fuel tank is not more than ¾ full.

8-16          TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481                                         15 March 2016

Rigging M929A2, 5-Ton Dump Truck on a 32-Foot Type V Platform for Low-Velocity Airdrop

                                                            1
                         5

                                                            2

① Store the mirrors, exhaust pipe, intake cape, spare tire tray, and taped nuts and bolts in the cab

     cover. Place all parts on the front seat and secure with ½-inch tubular nylon.

② Place the tailgate in the up position. Safety the tailgate with type III nylon cord on the hinges and

     chains.

③ Tie the hood shut, doors, spreading chains, dump release handle in the closed position, steering

     wheel, spread flaps, and hood latch bar with type III nylon cord (not shown).

④ Tape all lights and glass objects with pressure sensitive tape (not shown).

⑤ Pad and tape the exhaust pipe and air intake pipe with cellulose wadding and pressure sensitive

     tape.

⑥ Pad the front and rear of the oil pan with felt. Run a 15-foot tiedown lashing around the front

     and a 15-tiedown around the rear of the oil pan and main frame. Secure both lashings with D-
     rings and load binders.

⑦ Secure the ovm box and the air cleaner assembly by running a 45-foot tiedown lashing around

     the ovm box and air cleaner, up across both cab doors. Secure the lashing in the cab of the
     truck with a D-ring and a loadbinder.

⑧ Secure each fuel tank with a 15-foot tiedown assembly and cellulose wadding .

⑨ Construct the components of a cab support bar using a 7/16- by 1 ½- by 7 1/8-inch piece of

     steel, a ¾- by 1 ½ by 34-inch piece of steel, and a 7/16- by 1 ½ by 34-inch piece of steel.

Note. The 34-inch piece of steel must be 1 3/16-inch thick. A single piece or a combination of
pieces may be used to get the correct thickness. The unit will supply this item.

⑩ Weld the pieces of steel together as shown.

Note. The bar must be positioned from the right side of the truck.

⑪ Slide the bar in place with the 7 1/8-inch piece of steel up and with one end of the bar over

     each mainframe rail and the bar under the transfer link assembly support. The 7 1/8-inch metal
     plate must be closest to the left side of the truck.

⑫ Tie each of the bar securely in place with several turns of ½-inch tubular nylon webbing.

① Place a 12- by 80-inch piece of honeycomb across the hood of the truck. Place a 10- by 80-inch

     piece of lumber on top of the honeycomb. Position an Attitude Control Bar (ACB) on top of the 10-
     by 80-inch piece of lumber. Tie the ACB, lumber, and honeycomb with type III nylon cord in three
     places.

② Place four pieces of 12- by 12-inch honeycomb ond one piece of 12- by 12-inch plywood on the

     body of each side of the truck for support. Run a lashing around the main frame, over the plywood
     and through the square hole of the ACB. Secure the lashing with a D-ring and a load binder.
     Repeat on the other side of the truck.

③ Run a 15-foot lashing around the forward bar of the ACB and the front buper on both sides of the

     truck. Secure the lashing with a load binder and D-ring.

④ Run a 15-foot lashing around the aft bar of the ACB and the tire bracket on the right side of the

     truck. Secure the lashing with a load binder and D-ring.

⑤ Run a 15-foot lashing around the aft bar of the ACB behind the air intake pipe, and around the

     spare tire bracket on the left side of the truck. Secure the lashing with a load binder and D-ring.

INSTALLING AND SAFETYING SUSPENSION SLINGS

     8-15. Install and safety the suspension slings as shown in Figure 8-33.

      2                          1

① Form the front suspension sling by attaching an 11-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing sling

     to a 5 ½-inch link assembly. Fold an 11-foot, (2-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing sling in half.
     Attach the free ends to the opposite end of the 5 ½-inch link assembly. Place the looped end of
     the sling on the right spacer of a 3-point link assembly. Place a sling sleeve on the lower portion
     of the front slings and wrap the ends with pressure sensitive tape. Attach slings to the tandem
     links with large clevises.

② Form the center suspension slings by folding two 11-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing

     slings in half. Attach the free ends to two large clevises. Attach the clevises to the second and
     third suspension links. Rout e the bell of a large clevis through the loops of both 11-foot slings.
     Attach one end of a 9-foot, (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing sling to the left spacer of the 3-
     point link assembly. Attach the other end of the sling to the large clevis. Attach a 3-foot, (4-loop),
     type XXVI nylon webbing sling to the top spacer of the 3-point link.

                            Figure 8-33. Suspension Slings Installed and Safetied

Chapter 8

INSTALLING RELEASE SYSTEM

     8-19. Prepare and install the release system as shown in Figure 8-38.

                                             1
                                                                                                                                2

                       4
                                                  3

 ① Prepare an M-2 cargo parachute release assembly according TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-

       11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5. Attach the release assembly to the
       suspension slings and the cargo parachutes according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA
       SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5. Center the release assembly on top of the load.

 ② Fold the suspension slings and secure the folds with single turns of type I, ¼-inch cotton

       webbing.

 ③ Secure the top of the release assembly according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA

       SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5. Secure the bottom of the release assembly
       according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

 ④ Install the arming lanyard according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-

       010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.
                                        Figure 8-38. Release System Installed

8-48  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481  15 March 2016


---

                    Rigging M929A2, 5-Ton Dump Truck on a 32-Foot Type V Platform for Low-Velocity Airdrop

INSTALLING EXTRACTION SYSTEM

     8-20. Install the extraction force transfer coupler extraction system as shown in Figure 8-39.

                                                                                      2
                                                                              1

                                  5

                                 4

                          3

① Attach the type V Extraction Force Transfer Actuator (EFTA) mounting brackets to the Front

     mounting holes in the left platform rail.

② Install an actuator with a 28-foot cable to the EFTA mounting brackets according to TM 4-

     48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5.

③ Attach the latch assembly to the extraction bracket according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-

     11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5 Safety the release cable to the
     outside lashings with type I, ¼-inch cotton webbing.

④ Connect one end of a 9-foot (4-loop), type XXVI nylon webbing sling (deployment line) to the top

     spacer of the link assembly. Connect the free end to the center large suspension clevis on the 3-
     foot clustering slings.

⑤ Fold the excess deployment line and secure the folds in place with tape or type I, ¼-inch cotton

     webbing.

                                     Figure 8-39. Extraction System Installed

15 March 2016  TM 4-48.06 /TO 13C7-2-481  8-49


---

Chapter 8

PLACING EXTRACTION PARACHUTE

     8-21. Select the extraction parachute and extraction line needed using the extraction line requirements table
     in TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5. Place the extraction
     parachute and line on the load for installation in the aircraft.

INSTALLING PROVISION FOR EMERGENCY RESTRAINTS

     8-22. Select and install the provisions for the emergency aft restraints according to the emergency aft
     restraint requirements table in TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010 REV 1/TO 13C7-
     1-5.

MARKING RIGGED LOAD

     8-23. Mark the rigged and load according to TM 4-48.02/MCRP 4-11.3J/NAVSEA SS400-AB-MMO-010
     REV 1/TO 13C7-1-5 and as shown in Figure 8-40. Complete Shipper’s Declaration for Dangerous Goods.
     If the load varies from the one shown, the weight, height, center of balance, and parachute requirements must
     be recomputed.

EQUIPMENT REQUIRED

     8-24. Use the equipment listed in Table 8-1 to rig this load.
